hyperMILL® AUTOMATION Center

-- 10 of 302 --

1. Introduction
The
hyperMILL® AUTOMATION Center is a software application that makes repetitive,
manual steps easy to automate.
OPEN MIND Technologies AG has developed a seamlessly integrated and powerful auto-
mation tool that allows almost all functions in
hyperMILL® and
hyperCAD®-S to be automa-
ted or standardised. Work processes can be combined under the control of a script.
The following features distinguish the software:
• Full access to almost all
hyperMILL® functions
• Full access to almost all
hyperCAD®-S functions
• Additional functions
• Customisable scripts and input instructions
• Parameterisable scripts
• Make scripts easy to understand with custom images
• Downstream processes remain unaffected by the automation and can be performed
without restrictions
• No programming language necessary
• High degree of flexibility and universal usability
• Short training period
Licensing model
Version Acquisition Target group
hyperMILL® AUTOMATION Center BASIC All customers
Generally integrated, for prepara-
tory routine processes
hyperMILL® AUTOMATION Center ADVANCED Purchasea Script develop-

tion

purpose of developing scripts as
a service of OPEN MIND
aThe software is essentially offered in two versions, whereby the Runtime licence can be used together with the
Advanced licence.
hyperMILL® AUTOMATION Center
1

-- 11 of 302 --

2. User interface
hyperMILL® AUTOMATION Center allows functions to be called from
hyperMILL® and
hyperCAD®-S. In addition, there are further additional functions that are only available in
hyperMILL® AUTOMATION Center.
Procedure
A script combines functions into a sequence, to be run from top to bottom. The script
contains components, which in turn contain functions. Functions are available as template
commands to be inserted into a script. Components can contain subcomponents.
1. As a prerequisite, check under hyperMILL → Setup → Settings in Database whether the
databases, in particular the tool database, are selected correctly.
NOTE
Project-specific organisation of data is necessary for working with
the
hyperMILL® AUTOMATION Center. For this reason, the Projekt
option is enabled under Document → Directories in the hyperMILL →
Setup → Settings function.
2. Open script management . Create a new script . Enter a name. The script is
still empty. Close the dialogue.
3. Prepare a script in definition mode . To do so, create a component or a
subcomponent in a component in the left column. Arrange the functions in the
desired sequence.
To do so, select the function or pre-configured component in the right column and
insert it in the left column using the button. This is also possible by double-clicking
with the left mouse button or dragging and dropping.
Close the dialogue when the script is complete.
4. Specify the parameters for each function in the parameters table in the main dialogue.
To do so, switch to parameter definition mode . Disable this mode once all param-
eters have been defined.
5. Test the script and optimise if necessary. To do so, run the script in steps or in its
entirety . A single step is also possible by double-clicking the function with the left
mouse button.
6. Once the script is ready, it can be optionally inserted into the menu and/or toolbar. Go
to script management and open the toolbar definition dialogue .
7. Scripts can be run from hyperMILL → Automation → ....
Default folder for the
hyperMILL® AUTOMATION Center
Company-wide default setting
Select a folder under hyperMILL → Setup → Settings in Application under Default paths
in the AUTOMATION Center branch. It is advisable to set up the default path on the
hyperMILL® AUTOMATION Center server. A search is made there for the following data:
• ...\Apps: Scripts provided by OPEN MIND or custom scripts to be displayed in the
Customer applications branch. Preset folder. Another folder can be prioritised in the
configuration.
NOTE
These template commands and pre-specified script components may
only be referenced or inserted once per script.
hyperMILL® AUTOMATION Center
2

-- 12 of 302 --

• ...\CLAMPS: Fixtures that are stored in subfolders for each fixture system. The fixture
systems can be optionally arranged according to machine.
• ...\DATABASE: When working with several, different versions of
hyperMILL® AUTOMA-
TION Center databases, for example Color_table.xml, MacroDB.db, ToolDB.db
and Virtual_tool.vtx, it is advisable to store them here.
• ...\EXPORTED: Custom, compressed scripts for data exchange or data backup
• ...\FEATURE: Feature file folder for exported features
• ...\LIBRARY: CAD data categories with CAD model data
• ...\MULTIOFFSET: .cfg definition files for milling area multiple allowances
• ...\REPORTS: *.xsl report layouts
• ...\STOCKS: *.cfg stock definition files that are stored in subfolders for each stock
category.
• ...\variants: Global script data that is stored in subfolders for each script. Each time
the
hyperMILL® AUTOMATION Center is started, the local scripts are updated if neces-
sary. Switch off these updates using the Update runtime scripts option in Configuration →
Process application.
• ...\VB SCRIPTS: Store VBScript files categorisedin subfolders.
• ...\PYTHON SCRIPTS: Store Python script files categorisedin subfolders.
2022.2
Local default settings
In the folder C:\Users\Public\Documents\OPEN MIND\USERS\[Username]\Auto-
mationCenter\...
• ...\variants: Local storage location for scripts. Each time the
hyperMILL® AUTOMA-
TION Center is started, the local scripts are updated, if necessary, from the ...\var-

Continuous mode
Continuous mode runs through the entire script until a stop command is reached
or input is necessary.
Step mode
Step mode runs only the selected steps.

the parameter definition.
1

5
1 	Enter a short description of the purpose of the function, to be displayed in the
script overview.
2 	Input field
Enter values. Depending on the function, a string or a number may be permissi-
ble.
The coloured background of a cell provides information about its function:
• Cream yellow: A value must be entered in any case.
• White: Input can be made by the user during the script run if the script is
designed to stop for the input of a value.
• Grey: Entering a value is optional.
3 	Drop-down list
In certain functions, a drop-down list allows the user to select from a list of
already existing entries, such as a machine or a material, for example.
4 	Input button
All table cells with a double border are input buttons. Click the button and then
select an entity to automatically transfer colour, layer or vector. This can be used
to avoid manual entry of an RGB value, layer or vector.
5 	Link string
Clicking a table cell in the first table column copies a link to the information
in the table cell to the clipboard. This link string makes it possible to use this
information in other locations. It is only possible to modify the information at the
original location.
The options in the input field are also processed from top to bottom. This is used for some
functions, for example, in the Set layer visible/invisible function.

Inline functions
Processing of entries for options in functions. Insert using arrow icon on the right in table
row. Can be used for each option to carry out ‘local’ calculations, for example, to round
values, read in translations, and so on.
biggest Use biggest value
biggest(10:20:30) results in 30
calc The result of the calculation is always a whole number.
calc(1+2)
decimal Number of decimal places
decimal()
file Separate the pure file from a complete file name.
file()
ge Value greater or equal to
ge(10:10 ; true:false) results in true
gt Value greater than
gt(10:20 ; true:false) results in false
le Value lower or equal to
le(20:20 ; true:false) results in true
lead Pad with zeros before first number
lead(50:4) results in 0050
local Read in translation
local([Developer_name]|#[LOC_ID])
For [Developer_name], enter a name as the working name in case there is
no localisation file. For [LOC_ID], enter the correct ID of the *.loc file with
the translation.

Editor is used.

2022.2
Format: Control whether completed, licensed customer solutions should be displayed in the
format *.xml or *.hms. This setting can only be specified with a Developer licence. No
change may be made with a BASIC licence.
Functionality: Filter the scope of the functionality that is displayed in accordance with the
selected licence.
Display command names in Execute mode: Optionally display the function names in Exe-
cute mode – useful for test scenarios.
Display hidden parameters: Also display the parameters from the parameter definition for
which the lock (in the first column of the table) is closed.
Ask reset runtime parameters: While a script is executed, settings are made that the script
remembers, such as the selection of a particular machine. To work with the original settings
of the script again, select this option. When the script is called again, a prompt appears
asking whether the settings should be reset.
Ask closing AUTOMATION Center: Optionally confirm the closure of the AUTOMATION
Center.
Enable subscripts: Enable the creation of subscripts.
Run inside AUTOMATION Center (no execute mode): For analysis purposes, the AUTO-
MATION Center is run with its dialogues to process a script.

Note
Always load the CAD model into the document before executing a script.
The Undo and Redo functions are not supported. Useful background information: CAD
entities such as the geometry that specifies the fixture are converted into CAM entities.
This removes them from the CAD entity management. These converted entities can only be
deleted, for example, as a whole.

Component options
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
Type: Select a mode of behaviour for the component.
• Container
A component container is used for structuring. The functions contained are processed in
their specific sequence.
• Linked functions
Functions that have the blue icon can be linked. Other functions, for example, those
with loops, cannot be linked.
• Layer loop
Contained functions are executed for the filtered layer, for example, for the respective
current layer $CURRENT_LAYER#.
Filter layer: Filter instruction, for example, enter a part of layer names.
• Counted loops
Contained functions are executed x times. The current number of passes is in $CUR-
RENT_LOOP_INDEX#.
Number of repeats: Enter a number.
• Counted loops 2
Contained functions are executed Y times, for example, for setting up a double loop,
where a loop is passed through X times. And inside, a second loop is passed through Y
times for each pass. The current number of passes is in $CURRENT_LOOP_INDEX2#.
Number of repeats 2: Enter a number.
• Joblist loop
Contained functions are executed for joblists that meet the condition.
• Job loop and Job loop 2
Contained functions are executed for the filtered job, for example, for the respective
current job $CURRENT_JOB#.
Joblist filter: Filter instruction, for example, enter a part of a joblist name.
Filter jobs: Filter instruction, for example, enter a part of a job name.
Only compound jobs: Optionally execute only for
hyperMILL® compound jobs.
Only active jobs: Optionally execute only for active jobs. A job is active when the Create
NC file option of the General dialogue page is enabled in a job.
• Job tool loop
Runs through all jobs and makes one pass for each existing tool.
• Tool loop
Contained functions are executed for the filtered tool, for example, for the respective
current tool $CURRENT_TOOL#.
Tool filter: Run through for milling tools, turning tools or all tools.
Used tools only: Only run through for used tools.
• Feature loop
Contained functions are executed for the filtered feature, for example, for the respective
current feature $CURRENT_FEATURE_LOOP_INDEX#.
Feature filter: Only run through for drill features or pocket features.
• Frame loop
Contained functions are executed for the filtered
hyperMILL® frames, for example, for the
respective current frame $CURRENT_FRAME#.
Filter frame: Filter instruction, for example, enter a part of a frame name.
• File loop
To run through all files with a selected file extension extension in a selected folder.
hyperMILL® AUTOMATION Center
13

-- 23 of 302 --

• Entity loop
Go through all entities filtered out using Face colour and Face layer.
• Bookmark loop
Contained functions are executed for the states stored by the filtered bookmarks, for
example, for the respective current bookmark §CURRENT_BOOKMARK_LOOP_INDEX#.
Bookmark filter: Filter instruction, for example, enter a part of a bookmark name.
• Workplane loop
Contained functions are executed for the filtered workplanes, for example, for the respec-
tive current workplane §CURRENT_WORKPLANE_LOOP_INDEX#.
Filter workplane: Filter instruction, for example, enter a part of the name of a workplane.
• Conditional container
A stop occurs before the component container is processed. The user must decide
whether a condition applies. If the user chooses ‘Yes’, the functions within the container
are executed. If the user selects ‘No’, the container is skipped (in contrast to conditions in
other types of components, where there is no compulsory stop).
Question: Enter a question.
Condition: Check whether a script variable is <, >, = or ≠ to a value.
Error level: Specify which information should be displayed as a message and at what time.
• Off: No messages are output.
• Basic: Messages are only output for serious errors.
• Input: Messages are only output if the starting conditions necessary for the script step do
not exist or are not correct.
• Input / output: Messages are only output if the starting conditions necessary for the script
step do not exist or are not correct or no result or no correct result can be generated.
• Detailed: Debugging information is also output for testing purposes during software devel-
opment.
Always visible: Specify whether an inactive component should be visible as a result of a set
condition. Inactive components are normally hidden.
Cleaning up during scripting
When generating a script, right-click a script step while editing the parameter definitions to
select the following functions:
Clean references: When is script is generated, script variables are assigned values by
functions. When changes are made, that is, other ideas on how to proceed are introduced,
a particular script variable may still exist but the associated generating function may no
longer exist. This function searches for these lost references and deletes the ones that are
no longer used.
Reset references Script variables (that is, the references) can be replaced with their values
(that is, the parameters) separately for the layer, colour, folder, files and value.
Auto reference chain: The parameters can be replaced (again) with their script variables
separately for the layer, colour, folder, files and value.
Optimize all: Execute all three steps together.
Scripts as a toolbar and in the menu
Up to 30 scripts can be added to the menu under hyperMILL → Automation → ... and to the
Automation – toolbar or to two toolbars that are made available ‘across the entire company’.
Changes made to the menu and to toolbars are visible after restarting the software.

Complex loop
At the beginning, the type of loop is defined using a condition.
Template commands → Variable → Helper functions → Complex loop
Advanced
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
Type: Select a mode of behaviour for the component.
• Container
A component container is used for structuring. The functions contained are processed in
their specific sequence.
• Linked functions
Functions that have the blue icon can be linked. Other functions, for example, those
with loops, cannot be linked.
• Layer loop
Contained functions are executed for the filtered layer, for example, for the respective
current layer $CURRENT_LAYER#.
Filter layer: Filter instruction, for example, enter a part of layer names.
• Counted loops
Contained functions are executed x times. The current number of passes is in $CUR-
RENT_LOOP_INDEX#.
Number of repeats: Enter a number.
• Counted loops 2
Contained functions are executed Y times, for example, for setting up a double loop,
where a loop is passed through X times. And inside, a second loop is passed through Y
times for each pass. The current number of passes is in $CURRENT_LOOP_INDEX2#.
Number of repeats 2: Enter a number.
• Joblist loop
Contained functions are executed for joblists that meet the condition.
• Job loop and Job loop 2
Contained functions are executed for the filtered job, for example, for the respective
current job $CURRENT_JOB#.
Joblist filter: Filter instruction, for example, enter a part of a joblist name.
Filter jobs: Filter instruction, for example, enter a part of a job name.
Only compound jobs: Optionally execute only for
hyperMILL® compound jobs.
Only active jobs: Optionally execute only for active jobs. A job is active when the Create
NC file option of the General dialogue page is enabled in a job.
hyperMILL® AUTOMATION Center
46

-- 56 of 302 --

• Job tool loop
Runs through all jobs and makes one pass for each existing tool.
• Tool loop
Contained functions are executed for the filtered tool, for example, for the respective
current tool $CURRENT_TOOL#.
Tool filter: Run through for milling tools, turning tools or all tools.
Used tools only: Only run through for used tools.
• Feature loop
Contained functions are executed for the filtered feature, for example, for the respective
current feature $CURRENT_FEATURE_LOOP_INDEX#.
Feature filter: Only run through for drill features or pocket features.
• Frame loop
Contained functions are executed for the filtered
hyperMILL® frames, for example, for the
respective current frame $CURRENT_FRAME#.
Filter frame: Filter instruction, for example, enter a part of a frame name.
• File loop
To run through all files with a selected file extension extension in a selected folder.
• Entity loop
Go through all entities filtered out using Face colour and Face layer.
• Bookmark loop
Contained functions are executed for the states stored by the filtered bookmarks, for
example, for the respective current bookmark §CURRENT_BOOKMARK_LOOP_INDEX#.
Bookmark filter: Filter instruction, for example, enter a part of a bookmark name.
• Workplane loop
Contained functions are executed for the filtered workplanes, for example, for the respec-
tive current workplane §CURRENT_WORKPLANE_LOOP_INDEX#.
Filter workplane: Filter instruction, for example, enter a part of the name of a workplane.
• Conditional container
A stop occurs before the component container is processed. The user must decide
whether a condition applies. If the user chooses ‘Yes’, the functions within the container
are executed. If the user selects ‘No’, the container is skipped (in contrast to conditions in
other types of components, where there is no compulsory stop).
Question: Enter a question.
Loop condition: Check whether a script variable is <, >, = or ≠ to a value.
Type (alternative): Select alternative loop type.
Only active jobs: Optionally execute only for active jobs. A job is active when the Create NC
file option of the General dialogue page is enabled in a job.
Condition: Check whether a script variable is <, >, = or ≠ to a value.
Error level: Specify which information should be displayed as a message and at what time.
• Off: No messages are output.
• Basic: Messages are only output for serious errors.
• Input: Messages are only output if the starting conditions necessary for the script step do
not exist or are not correct.
• Input / output: Messages are only output if the starting conditions necessary for the script
step do not exist or are not correct or no result or no correct result can be generated.
• Detailed: Debugging information is also output for testing purposes during software devel-
opment.
Read translation file
Read in translations for script steps.
Template commands → Variable → Helper functions → Read translation file
Advanced
Read in translations for script steps.
hyperMILL® AUTOMATION Center
47

-- 57 of 302 --

Only the languages offered for
hyperMILL®and their language codes are supported:
de: German, fr: French, it: Italian, nl: Dutch, es: Spanish, cs: Czech, pl: Polish, ru:
Russian, sl: Slovenian, ja: Japanese, ko: Korean. zh_CN: Simplified Chinese, zh_TW:
Traditional Chinese, pt_BR: Brazilian Portuguese, tr: Turkish.
Prepare the translations in a text file in *.loc format. The text file must contain the
translations in the structure ID[TAB]TRANSLATION. Use a new line for each ID. There
must be a tab between the ID and translation!
Example 14. Localisation files for German, English and Spanish
project.de.loc
id01 Meine Elemente rot einfärben
id02 Meine Elemente blau einfärben
project.en.loc
id01 Colouring my entities in red
id02 Colouring my entities in blue
project.es.loc
id01 Colorear mis entidades en rojo
id02 Colorear mis entidades en rojo
Use the translation, for example, in a Command name, for descriptions, comments and
so on by entering the call local([Developer_name]|#[LOC_ID]). For [Develop-
er_name], enter a name as the working name in case there is no localisation file. For
[LOC_ID], enter the correct ID of the *.loc file with the translation.
Example 15. Adopt localisation
Table 1. Call
Command name: local(Colouring entity|#id01)
Table 2. Result for Spanish
Nombre de función: Colorear mis entidades en rojo

the script.

Start composite UI
Combine multiple script steps in one dialogue.
Template commands → Variable → Helper functions → Start composite UI
Advanced
Combine multiple script steps in one dialogue. However, only if the script is executed in
Execute mode. To do this, insert this function into a script at the beginning of the required
area.
There are two variants:
• Combine all inputs and outputs in one dialogue up to End composite UI. All inserted
script steps are processed. To do this, insert End composite UI at the end of the area.
• Combine all inputs up to the next stop for an input. Processing does not take place.
Start composite UI determines the preset, first image. Each script step can have its own
image. An image comes from End composite UI if no own images have been inserted.
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
End composite UI
Stop combining the inputs and outputs of multiple script steps in one dia-
logue.
Template commands → Variable → Helper functions → End composite UI

dialogue. To do this, insert this function into a script at the end of the required area. All
hyperMILL® AUTOMATION Center

is used as the name in the script.
Count colours: Select how many colours are displayed for selection within the dialogue.
Colour: Enter or select a colour with RGB values.
Alternating colours: Always use the next colour for each action. Start again with the first
colour after the last available colour.
Filter: Make selecting less prone to error. If known, restrict the selection to certain geometric
shapes, for example, only faces or only curves.
Selection constrain: Select Single selection, Tangentially, Fillet, Chamfer, Coplanar or Limi-
ted as the default selection option for the dialogue. All selection options are offered when
All is selected. Different parameters are offered, depending on the selection condition. Also
see Dialogue during the script run (page 53).

Each colour can be assigned its own image. Right-click to open a menu in a colour line:
Import image Insert an existing image in the format *.png, *.jpg or *.gif (also
animated GIFs).
Modify image Modify the image with Microsoft© Paint©.
Delete image Remove the own image. The original, preset image is displayed
again.

Search equal rotational faces
Search for and colour cone, torus or cylinder entities with the same geo-
metric properties.
Template commands → Colouring → Search equal rotational faces
Advanced
Search for all cone, torus or cylinder entities that have the same geometric properties as the
entity serving as the pattern.
The direction of the entities must also match.
The pattern entity is evaluated on the first call and the values for searching for further
entities are filled in. Based on this information, all identical segments are searched for on
the next call.
The pattern entity must be assigned a unique colour. All the entities found are assigned the
same colour as the pattern entity. Closed shapes are not allowed. Only specify the actual
faces as patterns.
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
Colour: Enter or select a colour with RGB values.
Min. segment angle: Enter a minimum segment angle.
Reference radius: Outputs the reference radius of the pattern entity.
Cone angle: Outputs the cone angle of the pattern entity. The cone angle is 0° for cylinders.
Second radius: Outputs the second radius of the pattern entity.
Z value for reference radius: Outputs the Z value for the position of the reference radius.
Type: Outputs the entity type that is determined. TORUS stands for torus-shaped segments
and CONE for conical segments. CONE is also output for cylinders – with a 0° cone angle.
hyperMILL® AUTOMATION Center

Preparation Contains different functions for preparing a CAD model and for importing files.
Select library
Select CAD data folder.
Template commands → Preparation → Select library
Advanced
Select a CAD data folder in order to be able to subsequently select CAD model data. See
Load model from library (page 71).
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
Library: From the drop-down list, select a folder in which the different CAD data of the
various categories is located.
NOTE
For each CAD data category, a ...\LIBRARY\[CADMODELTYPE] fold-
er must be located in the defined default path that is specified under
hyperMILL → Setup → Settings in Application under Default paths in the
AUTOMATION Center branch.
Stop for input: Execution of the script is halted so that information can be entered.
Load model from library
Select CAD model data.
Template commands → Preparation → Load model from library
Advanced
Select CAD model data for the selected CAD data category from a drop-down list. The
Select library function must be executed beforehand in order to determine the required CAD
data category. See Select library (page 71).
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
Models / Sketches: Select CAD model data.
Stop for input: Execution of the script is halted so that information can be entered.
Set layer visible / invisible
Change the visibility of existing layers.
Template commands → Preparation → Set layer visible / invisible
Advanced
Use a combination of Layer invisible and Layer visible to enable a specific selection of
layers. Use a combination of Layer visible and Layer invisible (second same-named option)
to specifically disable one or several layers. Separate multiple layer names with a semico-
lon ;.
hyperMILL® AUTOMATION Center
71

-- 81 of 302 --

Example 36. Only make a few layers visible
1. Use the first Layer invisible option to generally disable all layers, that is, make all
entities invisible. To do this, use the asterisk (*) symbol as a wildcard for all layer
names.
2. In the case of the Layer visible option, it is now simply necessary to enter all the layers
that should be enabled again. Separate multiple layer names with a semicolon ;.
Example 37. Only make a few layers invisible
1. Use the Layer visible option to generally enable all layers, that is, make all entities
visible. To do this, use the asterisk (*) symbol as a wildcard for all layer names.
2. In the case of the second Layer invisible option, it is now simply necessary to enter
all the layers that should be disabled again. Separate multiple layer names with a
semicolon ;.
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
Layer invisible: Enter or select which layer should not be visible and should therefore not
be evaluated. The asterisk (*) symbol can be used as a wildcard. To make a selection, first
click the button in the table cell and then select a layer entity in the graphic area.
The layer visibility control is performed on reaching the function and not on executing the
function.
Layer visible: Enter or select which layer should be visible and should therefore be evalu-
ated. The asterisk (*) symbol can be used as a wildcard. To make a selection, first click the
button in the table cell and then select a layer entity in the graphic area.
The layer visibility control is performed on reaching the function and not on executing the
function.
Layer invisible: Enter or select which layer should not be visible and should therefore not
be evaluated. The asterisk (*) symbol can be used as a wildcard. To make a selection, first
click the button in the table cell and then select a layer entity in the graphic area.
The layer visibility control is performed on reaching the function and not on executing the
function.
Stop for input: Execution of the script is halted so that information can be entered.

Position a part so that two cylinder axes are brought into alignment.
Template commands → Transformation → Positioning by coaxial cylinder
Advanced
Position a part so that two cylinder axes are brought into alignment. The positioning at
height is performed automatically. The lower edge of the source cylinder is placed on the
upper edge of the target cylinder. A suitable workplane must be chosen.
Also see Rotation by coaxial cylinder (page 89).

Move entities to reference height
Move entities to the height of a reference.
Template commands → Transformation → Move entities to reference height
Advanced
Move entities in the Z direction to the height of a plane reference face on another layer.
The layer of the entities to be moved and the layer of the entities to be compared must
be different. The colours of the faces to be aligned at the height must be the same. These
faces must be plane faces and have an aligned face normal vector. The layer of the entities
to be compared is not specifically selected here.
Example 51. Fit CAD model to stock
Align a stock on a layer 1 with a CAD model on a layer 2 in the Z direction.
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
Selection colour: Specify a suitable face colour and click the two faces that are to be
aligned.
Layer: Enter a layer on which the entities to be used are located.
Repeat entities on points
Repeat entities on positions that are specified via points.
Template commands → Transformation → Repeat entities on points
Advanced
Repeat entities on positions that are specified via points. The face normal vector, which
exists at the point, is considered for the alignment. The point must be located on the
reference face.
The basic entities must be located at the origin of the world workplane. Only a normal
direction is permitted.
Example 52. Distribute holes on cylinder face
Distribute a pattern hole on a cylindrical face at different positions so that the respective
hole is aligned in exactly the same way as the face normal vector of the face, where the
respective point is located.

Example 53. Restore holes on deformed face
If a model space, including the holes that exist there, were to be deformed, the holes would
also be deformed in an adverse manner. For this reason, determine the hole start points
in advance and remove the hole faces. Deform the faces along with the start points. Then
insert the holes again at their start points.
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
Base point layer: Enter reference points for the entities to be copied at the target 1 	.
Base entity layer: Enter the layer with the entities to be copied 2 	.
Normal face layer: Enter a layer with the reference face at the target. Only one face is
permitted. The direction of the copied entities is determined from the face normals 3 	.
Normal face colour: Specify the colour of the base face. Only one face is permitted. The
direction of the copied entities is determined from the face normals of this face at the target
reference point 4 	.
Target layer: Enter or select a layer for the entities that are generated or modified with this
function.
Tolerance: Enter the maximum permissible distance from the reference point to the refer-
ence face.
Invert direction: Invert the direction resulting from the face normals.

Basic
The colours of the CAD model are used to specify different tolerances. A definition file can
be edited in a text editor and different offsets can be assigned.
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
Milling area name: Enter or select a name for the milling area.
Definition files: In the drop-down menu, select a file from the list of files in the ...\MULTI-
OFFSET\ folder.
NOTE
An example is available in the ...\addins\hmAutoColor\Wiz-
ards\AutomationCenter\OMGEAPP\includeStart\... installa-
tion folder. The name of the example is ‘Multioffset.cfg’.
Save a definition file in the ...\MULTIOFFSET\ folder in order to make
it available to
hyperMILL® AUTOMATION Center. The folder is located
in the defined default path that is specified under hyperMILL → Setup →
Settings in Application under Default paths in the AUTOMATION Center
branch.
Example 54. Example of the syntax of a text file for defining multiple offsets
*START
*TARGET:="GroupSurfs0"
*ALLOWANCE:="0.0"
*ORIG_COLOR1:="95;0;0"
*ORIG_COLOR2:="255;175;175"
*ORIG_COLOR3:="0;175;175"
*ORIG_COLOR4:="255;255;0"
*ORIG_COLOR5:="255;0;255"
*ORIG_COLOR6:="239;239;239"
*ORIG_COLOR7:="255;175;0"
*END
*START
*TARGET:="GroupSurfs1"
*ALLOWANCE:="0.5"
*ORIG_COLOR1:="225;180;180"
*ORIG_COLOR2:="255;128;0"
*ORIG_COLOR3:="80;143;12"
*ORIG_COLOR4:="0;207;111"
*ORIG_COLOR5:="210;210;255"
*ORIG_COLOR5:="168;122;168"
*END

Milling area bounding box
Create a bounding box for the milling area that is defined in the last joblist.
Template commands → Start → Milling area bounding box
Basic
Create a bounding box at the outer edges of the milling area, relative to the NCS (not to the
workplane). 12 lines are created that are placed on the bounding box layer.
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
Bounding box layer: Enter a name for a layer on which to place the bounding box lines to
be generated.
Additional milling area
Create an additional milling area that is not automatically selected for the joblist.
Template commands → Start → Additional milling area
Basic
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
Milling area name: Enter or select a name for the milling area.
Allowance: Height of the allowance that will be used in the cycles with multiple allowances.
The value is added to the allowance that is defined within the cycle.
Face layer: Enter one or several layers that exist in the model whose faces should be inclu-
ded. Separate multiple layer names with a semicolon ;, for example, Layer1; Layer2.
Optionally click the button in the table cell and select a face of the respective layer. The
layer name is transferred.
Face colour: Enter an RGB colour value for the faces that exist in the model that are to
be included. Optionally click the button in the table cell and select a face of the respective
colour. The RGB colour value is transferred.
Layer invisible: Enter or select which layer should not be visible and should therefore not
be evaluated. The asterisk (*) symbol can be used as a wildcard. To make a selection, first
click the button in the table cell and then select a layer entity in the graphic area.
The layer visibility control is performed on reaching the function and not on executing the
function.
Layer visible: Enter or select which layer should be visible and should therefore be evalu-
ated. The asterisk (*) symbol can be used as a wildcard. To make a selection, first click the
button in the table cell and then select a layer entity in the graphic area.
The layer visibility control is performed on reaching the function and not on executing the
function.
Restore layer setting: Restore all layer settings to the state they were in before this function
was executed.

Basic
Set a frame limit. Macros are only applied if the features to be processed are located in a
defined angle range.
hyperMILL® AUTOMATION Center
107

-- 117 of 302 --

Use the frame limit to automatically assign jobs with hole features to a joblist. The assign-
ment is based on the direction of the hole features used in the job that can be defined using
an angle limit of the B and C axis.
Specify the permissible angle range of the B and C axis. Here, the B axis corresponds to
the Y direction and the C axis corresponds to the Z direction of the selected coordinate
system.
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
B axis min. value and B axis max. value: Specify a minimum and a maximum angle in the B
axis.
C axis min. value and C axis max. value: Specify a minimum and a maximum angle in the C
axis.
Stop for input: Execution of the script is halted so that information can be entered.

will be created for jobs whose feature orientation lies outside the defined frame limits.

Template commands → NCS → NCS from workplane
Basic
To achieve a correct result, a milling area is required on the current CAD model position.
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
Name of NCS: Enter a name.
Type: Select NCS or origin.
NCS position: Select a position from various positions of the milling area bounding box.
Possible positions:
• Bottom-Center
• Bottom-Center-Back
• Bottom-Center-Front
• Bottom-Center-Left
• Bottom-Center-Right

• Top-Left-Back
• Top-Left-Front
• Top-Right-Back
• Top-Right-Front
• Workplane
Direction: Select a direction for the Z axis. The direction is used for the NCS in the joblist.
Keep axis: Select an axis for which a current position is to be maintained.
Fixed X Position: The X value of the position is not changed. Therefore, if the X and Y
values are fixed, only the Z value will be modified.
Fixed Y position: The Y value of the position is not changed.
Fixed Z Position: The Z value of the position is not changed.

NCS best fit
Calculate an NCS based on the smallest dimensions of the CAD model.
Template commands → NCS → NCS best fit
Basic
Calculate an NCS based on the smallest dimensions of the CAD model. The position of the
CAD model to the world coordinate system does not matter.
A joblist with a milling area is required. The Z direction is based on the current view in
hyperCAD®-S.
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
Name of NCS: Enter a name.
With a pure 3D free-form model, no precise orientation using special planes is needed, that
is, no fine adjustment to planes is required. A fine adjustment is required for a prismatic
CAD model. A planes search takes place in the tolerance range. The planes search can be
optimised if a colour and a layer are already established for a plane to be used:
Fine adjust to planes: Specify whether the NCS should be finely adjusted using a plane with
a specific colour. To use the fine adjustment, a suitable, coloured, plane face must exist.
Fine adjust colour: Specify a colour for the fine adjustment using a plane. The colour of the
plane should only be provided for this purpose.
Layer: Enter a layer on which the entities to be used are located.
NCS position: Select a position from various positions of the milling area bounding box.
Possible positions:

Cylinder dimension
Generate a cylindrical
hyperMILL® stock with fixed dimensions.
Template commands → Stock → Cylinder dimension
Basic
Generate a cylindrical
hyperMILL® stock from diameter and height (or length). The stock is
aligned based on the NCS of the current job list and is inserted there. The top face of the
cylinder lies on the highest point of the selected faces, shifted by the stock position Z – if
defined. The rest of the CAD model is not taken into account.
Example 57.

Profile stock
Create a round stock that follows the profile of the model in the Z direction.
Template commands → Stock → Profile stock
Basic
The Z axis of the NCS is used as a basis. All faces that specify the model to be machined
should be on visible layers, all other face entities should be on invisible layers. The stock
offset enlarges the stock in all directions.

is used as the name in the script.
Stock name: Enter a name for the stock to be generated.

NOTE
The definition files must be located in a ...\STOCKS\[STOCKTYPE]

Setup → Settings in Application underDefault paths in theAUTOMATION
Center branch. If this folder does not exist, it must be created. [STOCK-
TYPE] should be replaced with another name (for example, the stock
group). Stock files are *.cfg files and can be edited with a text editor
and provided with various widths and heights.

X_WIDTH:= 42
Y_WIDTH:= 20
HEIGHT:=30
Example 59. STOCK40-30-35.cfg

Calculate stock dimensions
Calculate stock dimensions for the Auto box dimension function.
Template commands → Stock → Calculate stock dimensions
Basic
Calculate stock dimensions that are required in the Auto box dimension (page 126) function
as a subsequent function, otherwise the result will be wrong. The calculated values are
automatically transferred and displayed there. It is advisable to use a script variable as the
stock name as a script variable can be reused for the automatic stock dimensions.
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
Stock name: Enter a name for the stock to be generated.
Grid: Select a rounding value to make it possible to work with the size of the stock.
Min stock offset XY: Enter a minimum side offset in the X and Y direction of the NCS. The
real one may be larger due to the grid.
Min stock offset Z: Enter a minimum stock offset in the +Z and -Z direction of the NCS. The
real one may be larger due to the grid.
Tolerance: Enter a maximum permissible distance between the faces selected for genera-
tion as initial information and the stock facets.

result and displays the calculated values.

• Bottom-Center-Left
• Bottom-Center-Right
• Bottom-Left-Back
• Bottom-Right-Back
• Bottom-Left-Front
• Bottom-Right-Front
• Top-Center
• Top-Center-Back
• Top-Center-Front
• Top-Center-Left
• Top-Center-Right
• Top-Left-Back
• Top-Left-Front
• Top-Right-Back
• Top-Right-Front

Fixtures Contains various functions for importing a fixture into the CAD model and defining the
hyperMILL® clamping range.
The functions build on each other and must follow a basic order in the script:
1. Prepare CAD fixture data
The data must be prepared and stored in an *.hmc file in a fixture system folder.
The following data must be determined when the data is prepared:
• Reference point as a reference between fixture and the lower edge of the milling
area or the stock or the workplane
• Non-moving parts of the fixture
• Moving parts of the fixture, for example, left and right side of a vice
• Fixture system folder and fixture name
The scripts in Template commands → Process → Setup fixture are available for prepar-
ing CAD fixture data.
2. Select fixture system
Select a folder with the required fixture system:
Select fixture system
3. Load CAD fixture data
hyperMILL® AUTOMATION Center
130

-- 140 of 302 --

Select CAD fixture data and load into the current project using, for example:
Load fixture to stock, Load fixture to milling area or Load fixture to workplane
Or, combine with a search for the best fixture:
1. Find best fixture (from Excel)
2. Load fixture to stock (Parameter), Load fixture to milling area (Parameter) or Load
fixture to Workplane (Parameter)
4. Position fixture
Position the fixture in relation to the CAD model and clamp the CAD model:
Rotate fixture, Adjust Z level (two selected faces) and Adjust fixture to stock or Adjust
fixture to milling area
In the Manual adjust fixture function, the job steps for positioning the fixture are com-
bined in one function.
5. Specify as fixture area
Transfer as fixture area to the
hyperMILL® joblist:
Finish clamping
In contrast, the Fixture function can be used to generate a new fixture area directly from
CAD entities of the current project. The fixture area is added automatically to the last
available joblist.

Template commands → Fixtures → Select fixture system
Basic
Before CAD fixture data can be loaded into the current project, the folder with the required
fixture system must be selected. See Load fixture to stock (page 134).
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
Filter machine name: If No is selected, all subfolders in the .../CLAMPS folder are offered.
If Yes is selected, all subfolders in the .../CLAMPS/[MACHINETYPE] folder are offered.
In this case, organise the data in this additional folder structure.
In the active joblist, a machine must be selected in Postprocessor → NC files. The name of
the folder and the name of the machine selected in the active joblist must match.

various fixture systems are located.
NOTE

er must be located in the defined default path that is specified under
hyperMILL → Setup → Settings in Application under Default paths in the
AUTOMATION Center branch.

Find best fixture (from Excel)
Search for the best CAD fixture data with the help of an Excel table.
Template commands → Fixtures → Find best fixture (from Excel)
Basic
Search for the best CAD fixture data with the help of an Excel table. The minimum and
maximum size of the opening (ymin and ymax) and the x width (for example, of the clamping
jaws) and the name of each fixture are listed in the Excel table. A search is performed to
find the best suited fixture using this information and a search criterion. The name is output
as the result. The fixture with the smallest overhang is most suitable in X.

Adjust Z level (two selected faces)
Move the fixture to the model in the Z direction using two selected faces.
Template commands → Fixtures → Adjust Z level (two selected faces)
Basic
Move the fixture to the part, for example, the stock, using two selected faces. The function
is not executed automatically. Two faces must be selected within the function sequence.
One face should be a plane face of the fixture. The other plane face should be the under-
side, for example, of the CAD model. A check is performed to see whether one of the two
faces is located on a layer that is intended for the CAD fixture data.

Fixtures
Generate a new fixture area from CAD entities of the current project.
Template commands → Fixtures → Fixtures
Basic
hyperMILL® AUTOMATION Center
139

-- 149 of 302 --

Generate a new fixture area from CAD entities of the current project. The entities are selec-
ted by assigning them before to specific unique layers and/or have a colour only intended
for this purpose. The fixture area is added automatically to the last available joblist.
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
Fixture name: Enter the name of the fixture to load or use the script variable for the result
from the Find best fixture (from Excel) function.
Face colour: Enter an RGB colour value for the faces that exist in the model that are to
be included. Optionally click the button in the table cell and select a face of the respective
colour. The RGB colour value is transferred.
Face layer: Enter one or several layers that exist in the model whose faces should be inclu-
ded. Separate multiple layer names with a semicolon ;, for example, Layer1; Layer2.
Optionally click the button in the table cell and select a face of the respective layer. The
layer name is transferred.
Tolerance: Enter a maximum permissible distance between the faceted top of the polyhe-
dron model and the real model faces. Add
| METRIC to specify that this value only applies
to metric models. This value is converted in the case of INCH models. The procedure is the
same when
| INCH is added.
Layer invisible: Enter or select which layer should not be visible and should therefore not
be evaluated. The asterisk (*) symbol can be used as a wildcard. To make a selection, first
click the button in the table cell and then select a layer entity in the graphic area.
The layer visibility control is performed on reaching the function and not on executing the
function.
Layer visible: Enter or select which layer should be visible and should therefore be evalu-
ated. The asterisk (*) symbol can be used as a wildcard. To make a selection, first click the
button in the table cell and then select a layer entity in the graphic area.
The layer visibility control is performed on reaching the function and not on executing the
function.
Restore layer setting: Restore all layer settings to the state they were in before this function
was executed.

Single point from points
Generate a point in the middle of a curve or on corners of an imaginary
bounding box.
Template commands → Curves → Single point from points
Advanced
Generate a new point on a corner of an imaginary bounding box or on the middle point,
sorted by IDs. In this way, several points can first be created on a curve in order to then
obtain the middle of the curve with the middle point. Only if an odd number of a minimum of
three points is specified.
Example 67.
1. Use the Point on curve script step in advance to generate an odd number of points on
a curve.
2. Use layer and colour to select these points.
3. The ID of the middle point is used to generate a new point that lies in the middle of the
contour.
4. A bounding box is created internally around the selected points. Further points can be
created at its vertices. To do this, execute this function as often as required.
hyperMILL® AUTOMATION Center
183

-- 193 of 302 --

points. For the vertices, a bounding box is created around the selected points. Its corners
determine the positions of the points to be generated.

Create helix curves on a cylinder (barrel) whose central axis is aligned parallel to the Z axis
of the workplane.
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
Start angle: With reference to the X axial direction (0°), enter where the (first) helix curve
begins.
Optionally enter a pitch angle or a pitch. When both values are entered, the pitch angle is
used:
Pitch angle: Enter an angle for the pitch of the helix curves.
Pitch: Enter a value for the pitch.
Max. depth: Enter the depth or height of the helix curves.
Count: Enter the number of helix curves. The start position is 360° divided by the number of
curves.
Alternating: A next helix curve starts in the opposite direction.
Direction: Specify whether the (first) helix curve is created oriented to the right or left.
Face layer: Enter one or several layers that exist in the model whose faces should be inclu-
ded. Separate multiple layer names with a semicolon ;, for example, Layer1; Layer2.
Optionally click the button in the table cell and select a face of the respective layer. The
layer name is transferred.
Face colour: Enter an RGB colour value for the faces that exist in the model that are to
be included. Optionally click the button in the table cell and select a face of the respective
colour. The RGB colour value is transferred.
Target colour left: Select a colour for the helix curves in the left-oriented direction.
Target colour right: Select a colour for the helix curves in the right-oriented direction.
Target layer: Enter or select a layer for the entities that are generated or modified with this
function.

Feature Functions for processing
hyperMILL® features, Customised Process Features (CPFs) and
macros.
Hole feature recognition
Recognise a hole feature.
Template commands → Feature → Hole feature recognition
Advanced
2022.2
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
Use Generic Hole only: As a result of the recognition, only generic holes are created.
Create associative point: Display and edit point coordinates of hole features relative to a
selected frame.
Use colour table: If working with a
hyperMILL® colour table and the colours for threads, fits
and so on are stored in the colour table, activate the use of the colour table.
Use colour table only: Use colour table recognises all holes and the holes that are coloured
with colours from the colour table receive the additional parameters from the colour table. If
the Use colour table only option is selected, only the holes whose colour matches colours in
the colour table are adopted. Holes with colours, which are not defined in the colour table,
are not recognised.
Grouping feature: Group together several features with identical parameters.
Frame creation mode: 2D: For each hole direction, create a feature list and assign a frame
to it. 5X: Combine all recognised features into one feature list, that, however, has no frame
assigned to it. Mixed: If several holes have the same direction, a feature list is created for
this direction and a frame is assigned to it. All other holes are combined into one feature list,
but a frame is not assigned to this list.
Diameter limit: Enable this option when holes require a diameter limit.
Min. segment angle: All cylindrical faces with a segment angle greater than 270° are
recognised as holes. A hole that is cut open in the middle has a segment angle of 180°.
A rounded inner corner of a rectangular pocket has a segment angle of 90°. If the Min.
segment angle were set to less than 90°, every corner of a rectangular pocket with rounded
corners would be recognised as a hole.
Check sink: A check is carried out to see if sinks can be machined from the defined
direction.
Direction: Select the direction that is to be taken into account for the entities.
Layer invisible: Enter or select which layer should not be visible and should therefore not
be evaluated. The asterisk (*) symbol can be used as a wildcard. To make a selection, first
click the button in the table cell and then select a layer entity in the graphic area.
The layer visibility control is performed on reaching the function and not on executing the
function.
Layer visible: Enter or select which layer should be visible and should therefore be evalu-
ated. The asterisk (*) symbol can be used as a wildcard. To make a selection, first click the
button in the table cell and then select a layer entity in the graphic area.
The layer visibility control is performed on reaching the function and not on executing the
function.

-- 222 of 302 --

NOTE
The ...\FEATURE\[FEATURECLASS] feature folders must be lo-

Setup → Settings in Application under Default paths in the AUTOMA-

3. When compiling the script in

lect feature folder function is inserted as a script step before the Load features function.

is offered in the left column of the dialogue.
1 	Features available in the feature subfolder.
2 	Loading list with features to be loaded into
hyperMILL®.
NOTE
The sequence of features corresponds to the sequence of the macros to
be applied.
3 	Moves the selected feature to the start of the loading list.
4 	Moves the selected feature one position higher in the loading list.
5 	Adds a feature to the loading list.
6 	Removes the selected feature from the loading list.
7 	Moves the selected feature one position lower in the loading list.
8 	Moves the selected feature to the end of the loading list.

Load generic CPF

Advanced
2022.2
Load a CPF defined as a pattern (abbreviation for Customized Process Feature) with a
layer name adapted to the model situation. For this purpose, layer names of the exported
CPF file are changed before the file is imported and applied. This makes the CPF definition
generally usable, that is, generic.
Run the Select feature folder (page 211) function beforehand. Also see Load index CPF
(page 216).
Example 70. Match a pattern CPF to a clamping situation with four setups on a machine
table that are organised on four layers.
1. All model data for the four setups are organised on four layers named
Model1,
Model2,
Model3 and
Model4.
2. If not already available, define a CPF that serves as a pattern. In the filter definition for
a layer filter in Filter definition → CAD attribute → Use layer filter → Layer filter → Layer,

• Macro reference: The sort sequence from the macro is retained.

is used as the name in the script.
Joblist: Select a joblist for using the function.
Check: machine: Check for machine collisions. The machine limits are also checked.
Check: stock: Check for stock collisions.
Check: machine / model: Check the machine for model collisions.
Check: fixture: Check for fixture collisions.
Message: Enter message text for ‘Collision detected’.
Is collision free?: ‘True’ is output if no collisions were found. ‘False’ is displayed in the event
of collisions.
Exact collision check (SIMULATION Center)
Perform a collision check.
Template commands → Programming → Exact collision check (SIMULATION
Center)
Basic
Perform a collision check without displaying a simulation. The collision check is performed
according to the options saved for the collision check. Collisions found are classified. It is
possible to configure which types of collisions are to be reported/are reported. In case of
collision, start either the
hyperMILL® VIRTUAL Machining Center or the
hyperMILL® SIMU-
LATION Center. If a postprocessor is selected where the Virtual Machine option is not set,
hyperMILL® AUTOMATION Center
230

-- 240 of 302 --

the
hyperMILL® SIMULATION Center will be started. If the option is set, the
hyperMILL®
VIRTUAL Machining Center is started.
Definitions
The following applies to the terms used in the collision check message:
Collision: Collision between part, stock, fixture and machine or the non-cutting part of the
tool (the tolerance values are defined in the dialogue Collision check options → Limits for
part contact). An NC program with collisions cannot be approved.
Gouge: Violation of the part with the cutting area of the tool. An NC program with part
violations cannot be approved.
Contact: Permitted violation of the part with the tool tip (the tolerance values are defined in
the dialogue Collision check options → Limits for part contact). An NC program with contacts
can be approved.
IMPORTANT
If the cutting edge length is 0, the length of the tool tip corresponds to the
cutting tool part. If the cutting edge length is not equal to 0, the length of
the tool tip without cutting edge corresponds to the non-cutting tool part.
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
Joblist: Select a joblist for using the function.
CHECK: gouge: Report whether collisions have occurred that are classified as a component
violation. In the event of a component violation, the cutting part of the (milling) tool causes
damage to the workpiece, for example, during an infeed through the part. This renders the
workpiece unusable.
CHECK: touch: Report whether collisions have occurred that are classified as a component
contact. The tool tip (cutting area, non-cutting area of the tool, core) touches the workpiece.
Note: This collision check is only performed if the Limits for component contact are set in
the collision check options.
CHECK: collision: Report whether other collisions have occurred, that is, collisions that
cannot be classified as a component contact or component violation.
CHECK: limits: Report whether machining can be carried out within the axis limits, that is,
whether production is possible in the selected clamping position.
Start VM in case of danger: If at least one of the four checked collisions occurs, the
hyperMILL® VIRTUAL Machining Center or
hyperMILL® SIMULATION Center software is
started and the data is loaded. Investigate the situation interactively.
Message: Enter message text for ‘Collision detected’.
Is collision free?: ‘True’ is output if no collisions were found. ‘False’ is displayed in the event
of collisions.

Execute VBS script
Execute a VBS script.
Template commands → External automation → Execute VBS script
Advanced
To manage complex requirements, an externally generated script can be executed in
the scripting language VBScript, also called VBS (also see, for example, https://en.wikipe-
dia.org/wiki/VBScript). The software program necessary comes with the operating system
– it can be found in %windir%/system32/wscript.exe". The user does therefore not
need to do anything.
Use the Choose VBS script folder (page 235) function to select the storage location of the
VBS scripts in advance.
Example 73. Determine whether a numerical value is greater than, equal to or less than
another numerical value.
Input:
Description VBS Parameter ‘P1’: Value1 | Description VBS Parameter ‘P2’: Value2
Value1: 25 | Value2: 50
Software program:
REM VBscript example
If P1>P2 Then
returnVal1= "The value "+ P1+ " is bigger then "+ P2
ElseIf P1=P2 Then
returnVal1= "The value "+ P1+ " is equal then "+ P2
Else
returnVal1= "The value "+ P1+ " is less then "+ P2
End if
returnVal2="returnVal2 is currently not used"
returnVal3="returnVal3 is currently not used"
returnVal4="returnVal4 is currently not used"
Results:
Return from script: The value 25 is less then 50
hyperMILL® AUTOMATION Center
235

-- 245 of 302 --

Return from script: returnVal2 is currently not used
Return from script: returnVal3 is currently not used
Return from script: returnVal4 is currently not used
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
VBS scripts: Select a VBS script from the drop-down menu.
If this option is not offered, the VBS script folder has not been selected. Use the button at
the end of the line to open the VBS script in a text editor.
Description VBS Parameter ‘P1’ to Description VBS Parameter ‘P20’: First enter a descrip-
tion for the parameters. On the next run, a value can then be entered in each case.
Pass up to 20 parameters to the VBS script. For processing, the variables P1 to P20 must
be used in the VBS script, whereby the value from Description VBS Parameter ‘P1’ is
passed to P1 and so on.
Return from script: The results of the calculation in the function. Transfer to other functions
with the help of the script variables. To do this, the results must be passed within the VBS
script to variables with the names returnVal1 to returnVal4.

Example 74. Determine whether a numerical value is greater than, equal to or less than
another numerical value.
Input:
Description Python Parameter ‘P1’: Value1 | Description Python parameter ‘P2’: Value2
Value1: 25 | Value2: 50
Software program:
# Python script example
if P1 > P2:
returnVal1= "The value "+ str(P1)+ " is bigger then "+ str(P2)
elif P1 == P2:
returnVal1= "The value "+ str(P1)+ " is equal then "+ str(P2)
else:
returnVal1= "The value "+ str(P1)+ " is less then "+ str(P2)
returnVal2="returnVal2 is currently not used"
returnVal3="returnVal3 is currently not used"
returnVal4="returnVal4 is currently not used"
Results:
Return from script: The value 25 is less then 50
Return from script: returnVal2 is currently not used
Return from script: returnVal3 is currently not used
Return from script: returnVal4 is currently not used

Call subprogram script
Continue with another script. Call a script as a subroutine.
Template commands → External automation → Call subprogram script
Advanced
After the script has been called, the process continues in the parent script. In general, a
check is made at the end of each script run to see whether it was called as a subprogram
from a parent script. Where necessary, the process returns to the position of the calling
script.
Up to 20 values can be passed to the child process. The parameter names of these values
are $FUNCTION_PARAM1# to $FUNCTION_PARAM20#.
Command name: Enter a brief explanation of the purpose of the function. This explanation
is used as the name in the script.
Script name: Enter a script name as it appears in the drop-down menu for selecting a script
in the
hyperMILL® AUTOMATION Center.
Subscript: Select a subscript to be executed if a subscript is defined within the selected
script.

the first time in a second step) are added to the first column of the table as names and can
be assigned values:
Parameter 1 description to Parameter 20 description: Enter short descriptions for the pa-
rameters.
Condition: Check whether a script variable is <, >, = or ≠ to a value.

hyperMILL® AUTOMATION Center server is stopped,

Always on Do not limit the time period.
Start immediately.
Stop immediately.
Running the AUTOMATION Center on a server
The
hyperMILL® AUTOMATION Center can be run easily on a server. The script must be
adapted so that there is no user interaction, otherwise the application will fail. It is very
important to know that the application only monitors one folder and executes a script that
has been defined beforehand. Every action such as ‘save file’, ‘log message’, ‘error’ must
be managed in the script. The application only executes what has been requested.
All files can be found in C:\Program Files\OPEN MIND\hyperMILL\[ver-
sion]\addins\hmAutoColor.
Configuring the PW Server (PWserver.exe)
An executable file must be defined:
• Watch folder
• Project number (script to be executed)
• Priority
• File type
The Watch folder must be a folder that is accessible to all clients and the server.

The log file must have the extension *.PWlog. The PW Monitor is started by the client.
The script manages everything that is entered into the PW Monitor and when it is entered.

-- 266 of 302 --

Using the PW Server with Excel files
Configuration file
Before starting, some parameters have to be defined. These parameters can be de-
fined in the autoProgramConfig.pcf file. This configuration file is located at C:\Pro-
gramme\OPEN MIND\hyperMILL\[Version]\addins\hmAutoColor. In this exam-
ple, the version is 29.0.
Three parameters must be defined:
*EXCEL_FOLDER=c:/temp/defaultExcel.xlsx
*DEFAULT_TEMPLATE=c:/temp/HCS_template.hmc
*DEFAULT_TEMP_START_FILE=c:/temp/start.hmc
The EXCEL file located in the server’s internal folder is copied to the name defined in the
parameter *EXCEL_FOLDER.
The file defined in *DEFAULT_TEMPLATE is copied to the file defined in *DE-
FAULT_TEMP_START_FILE.
hyperCAD®-S is started with *DEFAULT_TEMP_START_FILE.
In the script, the user is to read the EXCEL file from *EXCEL_FOLDER. It is assumed that a
script name for the process has been defined in it. It must be called from the start script.
A model file is to be found in EXCEL that is to be merged with the current model.
The file should be saved in a location defined in the EXCEL file.
The file must be edited as administrator.
The file configured as an example:
hmct document template
It is possible to define a
hyperCAD®-S template to be used in the process. Define layers,
colours and so on in this template. The
hyperMILL® AUTOMATION Center copies the
template to DEFAULT_START_FILE and starts based on this file.
hyperMILL® AUTOMATION Center
257

-- 267 of 302 --

Excel file

• Use File path to define the location of the 3D model to be merged.
• Use File to define the 3D model to be merged.
• Use Script to define the script to be started.

Excel calculation Contains functions for passing values to Microsoft Excel for the purpose of performing
calculations there or for importing data from Microsoft Excel.
For example, in the function Write value in Excel, why not ‘...save’?
A file is opened and data is read out. Conversely, data is written to Excel. The file is only
saved once all the data has been written. The file is opened automatically if it has not
already been opened. There is a function for closing the file.

Read all parameters from XML file
Read all parameters from XML file.
Template commands → XML Files → Read → Read all parameters from XML file
Advanced
The result of reading the file is placed in script variables. The name of the script variable
is formed from the data set name, segment name, function name and parameter name, for
example, $Segment::Function:Parameter1#. The name of the XML file is inserted in
the parameter description, for example,
Read from XML:Example.
This variable and its value are only temporarily available after reading has taken place. As a
result, this information is only available for the current script run.

Explorer).

Example 87. Determine number of tools

for example, the number of passes of a tool loop.

<toolHolders />

<tools folder="TT1100 Axial grooving tools">

<tool type="recessingTtool" name="318842 B3.1"/>
<tool type="recessingTtool" name="318842 B3.2"/>
<tool type="recessingTtool" name="318842 B3.3"/>
</tools>
</tools>
</tools>
</omtdx>
